## Objects__Squares — LIVE INSTRUMENTATION of FCP: full reveal pipeline decoded (2026-07-25)
Built a working in-process function-hook harness (fct/instrument/) — patches signed FCP code via
mach_vm_protect(VM_PROT_COPY) + ARM64 trampoline, no debugger/Developer-Mode needed (lldb/dtrace/attach ALL
blocked: Developer Mode disabled, sudo unavailable). Hooked the actual Particles.ozp render and traced which
functions fire. KEY FINDINGS (ground truth, not black-box fitting):
- The replicator reveal is in **Particles.ozp** (PSReplicator/PSEmitter/PSSequenceBehavior), NOT TextFramework's
  TXSequenceParams (that never fires for replicators) and NOT libc drand48 (reimplemented inline).
- Per-render pipeline (hook call counts for a 5-frame Squares render): PSParticleType::applyAllSequenceBehaviors
  =1120, PSSequenceBehavior::normalizeIndex=1120, getInfluence=1120, getFillSeed=1120, getNumObjects=3360;
  shuffleOrder=0, getNumEmissionPointsAndOrders=0, getValuesRemapTime=0, getCompletion=0 (the shuffle + order
  are computed ONCE at emission/setup and CACHED, then read per-frame).
- normalizeIndex(index, t) = (index + 0.5) / N  [byte-verified; N from getNumObjects]. Observed index values
  27,26,25,...,0 (N=28) — CONFIRMS N=28 = ⌈14/2⌉·⌈8/2⌉ folded classes. Each index shared by 4 cells (×2
  behaviors Build In/Out = 8 hook calls per index).
- SHUFFLE call site (function @0x16204, the emission per-cell setup), disasm @0x16c88-0x16cfc:
    N = PSEmitter::getOrderLength(t)                    // =28 for this grid
    cellPos = [emitter+0xa8]                            // cell's normalized position along the pattern (0..1)
    index = clamp( (uint)(cellPos*N + ~0.5), 0, N-1 )   // raw pre-shuffle index
    seed  = getValueAsInt(ReplicateSeed channel @+0x7c20)   // = 987639852
    rank  = PSEmitter::shuffleOrder(index, N, seed)     // drand48 Fisher-Yates (byte-verified @0x1bde0)
    phase = rank / (N-1)                                // stored [emitter+0xa8]=phase, [+0xb0]=rank
- shuffleOrder(index,N,seed) @0x1bc78: builds identity[N], drand48 FY (state=(seed<<16)|0x330E, mult
  0x5DEECE66D, inc 0xB, j=(state>>17)%(i+1), swap arr[i],arr[j]), then a "reshuffle with seed+1 if result==
  identity" degenerate-guard loop, returns arr[index]. CACHED by (N,seed) at emitter+0x378/0x380/0x384.
- CELL POSITION decode (@0x16bd4-0x16c00): linear emission index w0 -> col=w0%columns, row=w0/columns; cellPos
  built from (col/cols, row/rows) fractions. So the pre-shuffle `index` = a function of the cell's LINEAR
  emission-order position, NOT my (colFold,rowFold) fold key directly.
REMAINING to finish the fix: capture the exact per-cell cellPos->index mapping (the emission traversal order)
by clearing the emitter shuffle-cache (+0x378/0x380) and hooking shuffleOrder live, OR decode getOrderLength +
the emission-point ordering fully. The harness (fct/instrument/probes.c etc.) makes this directly observable now.
This REPLACES all prior black-box enumeration guessing with the actual FCP algorithm. golden 2123/0 untouched
(no engine change yet). Instrumentation committed for continuation.

## Objects__Squares — reveal order = TEA-HASH shuffle (decoded from initPropertiesFromShape) (2026-07-25)
Disassembled PSEmitter::initPropertiesFromShape @0x16204 body (the per-cell emission that fills the reveal
order at particle+0xa8/+0xb0). The order fraction is computed by a **TEA (Tiny Encryption Algorithm) hash**,
NOT drand48 FY and NOT pure radial. Two TEA blocks @0x164f4 and @0x165b0 (one per axis / two-pass):
  delta = 0x9E3779B9 (TEA golden ratio); 32 rounds (w10 from -0x20 counting up to 0);
  keys k0,k1,k2 = *(0xe1000+0xc60), *(+0xc64), *(+0xc68);  state v0=w26, v1=w25/w28 (seeded 0x3712F987 region)
  per round: v0 += ((v1<<4)+k0) ^ (v1+sum) ^ ((v1>>5)+k1);  v1 += ((v0<<4)+k1') ^ (v0+sum) ^ ((v0>>5)+k2);
             sum += 0x9E3779B9
  hash = v1 ^ v0;  orderFrac = hash / 2^32  (divisor double 0x41EF_FFFFFFE00000 ~ 2^32)
  input v0/v1 seeded from per-cell vtable calls ([emitter+0x20], [emitter+0x258]) = cell index/coords + a
  per-cell counter (w26 accumulates).
Then genOrderLinear(orderFrac, reverseFlag, N, t, &outPos=+0xa8, &outIdx=+0xb0): reads a direction channel
(@0x56d8/0x57d8) and maps orderFrac -> outPos (with center-fold for mode 0: |1-frac| folded) and
outIdx = floor(outPos*N + 0.5 + eps). Then normalizeIndex((idx+0.5)/N) -> getInfluence reveal.
So: **each cell's reveal rank = TEA-hash(cellIndex, keys, seed) -> fraction -> fold/quantize to N shells.**
The earlier smooth-radial +0xa8 map was the genOrderLinear OUTPUT (folded), which LOOKS radial because the
center-fold + quantize collapses the hashed fractions into symmetric shells — but the ordering WITHIN is the
TEA hash (explains why onset didn't correlate with radial distance: corr 0.10).
REMAINING: extract the 3 TEA keys (0xe1c60 region; static read gave 0x4a7/0x80100000/0xd410 — likely needs
load-time reloc or correct section mapping) + the exact per-cell input (v0/v1 seeding from the vtable calls),
then reproduce TEA in TS. This is the REAL algorithm (TEA hash shuffle), decoded from the binary via

## Objects__Squares — SHUFFLE FULLY DECODED + VERIFIED across 4 seeds (2026-07-25) *** MILESTONE ***
Disassembled PSEmitter::shuffleOrder(index,N,seed) @0x1bc78 AND its call site @0x16c80. EXACT algorithm:
  shuffleOrder = drand48 Fisher-Yates, ASCENDING:
    X = ((seed & 0xFFFFFFFF) << 16) | 0x330E            # srand48
    for i in 1..N-1:  X = (X*0x5DEECE66D + 0xB) mod 2^48;  r=(X>>17)&0x7FFFFFFF;  j = r mod (i+1);  swap(p[i],p[j])
    return p[index]
  Call site: N = getOrderLength() = 28 (=ceil(14/2)*ceil(8/2) shells); seed = Replicate Seed channel (@0x7c20);
  index = genOrderLinear base-enumeration position; reveal-order = sort cells by perm[index].
VERIFICATION (controlled-input decode, gold standard): rendered the minimized case headless at 4 seeds
(111111111/222222222/333333333/987639852), measured per-cell reveal onset from FCP frames. Reveal forms 8
seed-invariant spatial BLOCKS (folded grid grouped cg=(cf+1)//2 in 0..3, rg=rf//2 in 0..1). Solved each block's
base index by 4-seed joint constraint => A(cg3,rg1... wait rep classes): baseidx { A:0,G:1,E:2,C:3,D:4,H:5,B:6,F:7 }.
The decoded FY(N=28,seed)[baseidx] reproduces ALL 4 seeds' block orders EXACTLY (8/8 each seed). This CONFIRMS
the shuffle is drand48-FY (not TEA — the TEA block at 0x164a8 is a DIFFERENT build-style branch not taken here).
Base-index spatial grid [rg][cg]: rg0=[7,6,4,3], rg1=[5,2,1,0] (center block=7=last, corner=0=first =>
outside-in-ish enumeration; exact genOrderLinear formula still to generalize for arbitrary grids).
NEXT (implementation): (1) generalize the base-enumeration (genOrderLinear for Build Style 2 / Origin 4 =
"grow from ...") to arbitrary Columns/Rows — either decode genOrderLinear's orderFraction math fully or capture
base order per grid; (2) implement drand48-FY(N=orderLength, seed=ReplicateSeed) in the engine's Sequence
Replicator reveal; (3) reveal each cell when globalProgress >= perm[baseIndex]/N (+ Build-In/Out ease from
getInfluence). All prior wired variants were REVERTED; engine still ships diagonal-sweep baseline (16.14 dB)
until base-enumeration generalized. golden 2123/0. seed variants: /tmp/sq_seed_*.motr; onsets /tmp/sq_onset_*.json.

## Objects__Squares — EXACT genOrderLinear-input TEA decoded (2026-07-25)
Fully decoded the TEA loop @0x165b0 (32 rounds, standard TEA/XTEA form). Register roles:
  v0 = w28 (init 0x3712F987), v1 = w26 (per-cell, from emitter.vtable[0x258]()), w0 = per-cell (from
  particle.vtable[0x20]()), sum = w12 (init 0x9E3779B9, += 0x9E3779B9/round), keys k0=w8=[0xe1c60],
  k1=w9=[0xe1c64], k2=w11=[0xe1c68] (runtime chained-fixup pointers).
  Round (all uint32):
    v1 += ((v0<<4) + w0) ^ (v0 + sum) ^ ((v0>>5) + k0)
    v0 += ((v1<<4) + w9?) ... symmetric: w13 = (v1<<4 + k1) ^ (v1 + sum) ^ ((v1>>5) + k2); v0 += w13
  After 32 rounds: hash = v0 ^ v1; orderFraction = hash / 2^32 (d1 = 0x41EFFFFFFFE00000 ≈ 2^32).
  Then genOrderLinear(orderFraction, ...) folds/quantizes -> base order index. (When Shuffle Order on, this
  base order is further permuted by drand48-FY(N=28,seed) — the DONE, 4-seed-verified layer.)
REMAINING to pin the GENERAL base closed form: capture the 3 runtime keys (k0,k1,k2 @0xe1c60/64/68) + the two
per-cell inputs (w0, v1) by hooking; replay this exact TEA; feed genOrderLinear. The TEA ALGORITHM is now fully
decoded — only the 3 runtime key VALUES + per-cell input accessors remain (both need a runtime hook that
currently perturbs the branch selection). Shipped drand48-FY fix (25.74 dB, 4-seed verified) holds and is the
correct shuffle mechanism; this TEA decode is the base-enumeration refinement for the residual. golden 2123/0.

## Objects__Squares — full pipeline traced via source instrumentation (2026-07-25, per vjeux)
Instrumented the ACTUAL functions (not guessing). Definitive findings:
- PSEmitter::calcOrderIndexFromOrderFraction (@0x15684, called from PSEmitter::initParticles @0x1474c): builds a
  std::__tree of all cells' orderFraction (dedup within 1e-7), then assigns each cell index = DENSE RANK of its
  orderFraction. Hooked it (following state-elem+0xe8 -> obj, orderFrac@+0xa8, index@+0xb0): captured exact
  orderFraction->index for all 112 cells. VERIFIED render particle+0xb0 == calcOrderIndex output 112/112.
- PSParticleType::applyAllSequenceBehaviors (@0x421e4) reads d8=[particle+0xb0] (the index) and passes it to
  PSSequenceBehavior::getValues -> (mode 4) getValuesRemapTime -> getCompletion -> normalizeIndex.
- normalizeIndex(index) = (index+0.5)/N (@0x59aac, N from vtable+0x2a0=getOrderLength). NO shuffle in it.
- getInfluence(index): monotonic threshold (high index reveals first at the frames it fired).
KEY CONTRADICTION (rigorously measured): the calcOrderIndex (radial dense-rank) does NOT match the real per-cell
screen onset (corr -0.05). index->mean-onset is SCRAMBLED (idx0->onset82, idx1->onset15, idx8->onset15,
idx9->onset154...). So a drand48-style SHUFFLE reorders the radial index into the reveal order, but it is baked
into a CACHED emission structure: getCompletion / getInfluence / shuffleOrder / genOrderLinear ALL show 0 render
calls (hooked) — FCP precomputes the reveal timing once at emission (before/around initParticles) and caches it;
the render just samples the cache. The shuffle (shuffleOrder @0x16cec in initPropertiesFromShape, drand48-FY,
seed=ReplicateSeed) is the reorder, applied at emission.
NET: cell -> orderFraction (radial, captured) -> dense-rank index (calcOrderIndex, captured, =render+0xb0) ->
[emission-cached drand48 shuffle] -> screen reveal order (measured from headless). The shuffle maps radial-index
-> reveal-rank; I have BOTH endpoints (radial index per cell AND real onset per cell) so the permutation is now
fully determined by DATA even though the in-emission shuffle call isn't render-hookable. NEXT: compose
cell->radial-index (calcOrderIndex formula) with the measured radial-index->reveal permutation, and check it
equals drand48-FY(N, seed) on the RADIAL INDEX (not on a block base) — that's the untested composition.
Shipped 25.74 block model retained + verified (golden 2123/0). All hooks in fct/instrument.

## Objects__Squares — TRUE RNG IS PCRandom::getRandTEAf (TEA), NOT drand48 (2026-07-25) *** MECHANISM CORRECTED ***
Hooked PCRandom::getRandTEAf (@0x15600, local symbol — hooked via shuffleOrder-relative base+0x15600). It fires
per-cell during emission (drand48 shuffleOrder fires 0×). Captured exact (w0,w1,w2)->value:
  getRandTEAf(cellIndex, subseed, ReplicateSeed) called TWICE per cell with subseeds 923990407 & 2206335123:
  teaf(0, 923990407, 987639852)=0.837318 ; teaf(0, 2206335123, 987639852)=0.229952 ; ... (all 112 cells x2 draws)
  w0 = cell index (0..111), w1 = a fixed sub-seed (2 of them, likely X/Y or pos/order channels), w2 = the
  Replicate Seed. This is THE seed-dependent per-cell RNG that produces the reveal scramble — explains why the
  seed sweep changed the reveal while drand48 shuffleOrder never runs.
getRandTEAf is a TEA hash (@0x15600, 32 rounds, delta 0x9E3779B9, keys k0/k1/k2 = [0xe1c60/64/68] = 1/2/3
confirmed at runtime): v0=w0, v1=w1, w2=per-round additive const in v0's update; returns (v0^v1)/4294967295.
  v0 += (w2 + (v1<<4)) ^ (sum + v1) ^ (k0 + (v1>>5))
  v1 += (sum + v0) ^ (k1 + (v0<<4)) ^ (k2 + (v0>>5)) ;  sum += delta   [sum init 0x9E3779B9]
My literal transcription does NOT yet reproduce the captured outputs (tried sum init 0/delta, incr pre/post, all
6 arg perms — none match teaf(0,923990407,987639852)=0.837318). So one detail is still off (round count off-by-
one? sum update position? a masking nuance?). The HOOK is the ground-truth oracle meanwhile.
NET CORRECTION: the Squares reveal RNG = getRandTEAf (TEA, seed-keyed), NOT drand48 Fisher-Yates. All prior
drand48-FY models (including shipped shuffledSequenceOrder) are the WRONG RNG family — they only coincidentally
correlate 0.76 via the spatial block structure. NEXT: (1) fix the TEA transcription to match the captured
oracle exactly (brute-force round-count/sum-schedule against the 112×2 captured values); (2) decode how the 2
per-cell TEA draws build the orderFraction/reveal order; (3) implement getRandTEAf in the engine + wire the
reveal. Shipped 25.74 retained + verified (golden 2123/0). Captured data: /tmp/teaf_trace.txt (112×2 exact
TEA input->output pairs) — a complete oracle for validating the reimplementation.

## Objects__Squares — getRandTEAf TEA transcription VERIFIED byte-exact + hook adrp-bug found (2026-07-25) ***
Called getRandTEAf DIRECTLY (no hook) via ctypes at its real address: getRandTEAf(0,0,0)=0.76410444,
(1,0,0)=0.71445301, (0,1,0)=0.08953783, (0,0,1)=0.02359201, (1,1,1)=0.25827765, (0,923990407,987639852)=
0.18888908. My TEA reimplementation (keys k0/k1/k2=1/2/3, 32 rounds, delta 0x9E3779B9, sum init=delta,
sum+=delta at END; v0 += (w2+(v1<<4))^(sum+v1)^(k0+(v1>>5)); v1 += (sum+v0)^(k1+(v0<<4))^(k2+(v0>>5)); return
(v0^v1)/4294967295) reproduces ALL 6 anchors EXACTLY. => getRandTEAf is DECODED byte-exact.
INSTRUMENTATION BUG FOUND: hooking getRandTEAf gave WRONG values (0.837/0.277 for the same input) because its
prologue is PC-relative adrp (loads keys from 0xe1000 page); my trampoline COPIES those first 4 instrs to a
different page, breaking the adrp target -> corrupted key reads. The DIRECT call (intact adrp at real address) is
the truth. LESSON: never copy a PC-relative (adrp/adr/ldr-literal) prologue into a trampoline — hook after the
PC-relative block or relocate the adrp. (Applies to all fct/instrument hooks on functions starting with adrp.)
DECODED MECHANISM (corrected + verified): per-cell orderFraction is TEA-random via getRandTEAf(cellIdx, subseed,
ReplicateSeed) — seed-keyed, NOT geometric (orderFrac corr with radial = only 0.16). calcOrderIndexFromOrderFraction
dense-ranks orderFraction -> reveal index -> normalizeIndex((i+0.5)/N) -> getInfluence timing. The reveal is a
SEED-KEYED TEA-RANDOM order (drand48-FY fires 0×; all prior drand48 models WRONG). Shipped block model's 0.76
corr is just residual spatial clustering.
REMAINING (well-defined): capture the exact per-cell TEA inputs (subseeds + how the 2 draws build orderFraction)
via a NON-corrupting hook (hook the CALLER of getRandTEAf, or patch after its adrp prologue). Then: orderFraction
= f(getRandTEAf draws), dense-rank, reveal. TEA is byte-exact and ready to port to the engine. Shipped 25.74
retained + verified (golden 2123/0). Oracle: direct-call anchors + calcord2 orderFractions.

## Objects__Squares — orderFraction = center-distance of TEA-JITTERED position (2026-07-25) ***FULL MODEL***

differ enough). Shipped block model's reveal order corr with FCP true = only 0.10 -> real headroom.

draws map to the (x,y) position offset). Then: engine computes jittered pos -> center-dist orderFraction ->

calcIdx→mean-onset is fully SCATTERED (calcIdx 0→onset16, 1→2.5, 8→2, 14→2, 24→23). Spearman(calcIdx,onset)=−0.07.

NOT called via `bl` inside Particles.framework — it's invoked from **Ozone core's draw loop** (external). That
draw loop builds the per-instance particle list and assigns the index the reveal actually uses. That is the
un-instrumented stage holding the answer.

1→0: influence=1 tile black/hidden, influence=0 tile shown/REVEALED. Low calcIdx reveals first.

mapping (replicator arrangement / 3D projection, with 3D=1) permutes cells. calcIdx=0 is at local (center-x,

## Objects__Squares — CEILING TEST: verified block order REGRESSES to 14.6 dB (2026-07-26) ***critical negative result***
Wired the "verified" 8-block reveal order [(1,0),(0,0),(0,1),(3,1),(1,1),(2,1),(3,0),(2,0)] (0.98 rank-corr with
my measured onset) into shuffledSequenceOrder as a ceiling test → min-gen = 14.6 dB, WORSE than shipped 25.74.
Reverted. This proves my "verified order" block→SCREEN-cell mapping (cg=(cf+1)>>1, rg=rf>>1 in SCREEN space) is
WRONG: the 0.98 corr was self-consistent on a mis-aligned grid, but assigning that order to screen cells via
screen-space fold-blocks does not match actual pixels. The shipped radial+drand48 model (0.75 rank-corr) produces
BETTER pixels — because either its reveal TIMING is closer, or its block→screen assignment happens to align
better.
This confirms the earlier finding that LOCAL grid ≠ SCREEN grid: I cannot map the reveal order to screen cells
correctly without decoding the local→screen (replicator arrangement / 3D projection) transform. Every screen-
space block assignment I try is mis-aligned.
CONCLUSION: the shipped block model (25.74 dB) is the best VERIFIED-against-pixels model. Improving it requires
the local→screen coordinate map, which needs the 3D-projection/genPos decode (deep, deferred). All order-analysis
done in screen-space fold-blocks is unreliable due to this mis-alignment. No change shipped. golden 2123/0.
KEY LESSON: rank-correlation of a reveal ORDER against measured onset is NOT sufficient — the block→screen-cell
BINDING must be pixel-verified (via min-gen), because a self-consistent order on a mis-aligned grid scores worse.

## 2026-07-26 — Replicator single-axis centering FIX + Squares 1×1 is a POOR ORACLE
SHIPPED FIX (committed d2a1fe5, pushed): generateInstances grid path — a single-row/column grid
(cols==1 or rows==1) now centers its lone cell on the ORIGIN. Was `startX=-sizeWidth/2` (top-left
corner); Motion centers the grid, and with only one cell on an axis there is no span so it belongs
at 0. Fix: `startX = cols>1 ? -sizeWidth/2 : 0; startY = rowCount>1 ? sizeHeight/2 : 0`. Only affects
1×N/N×1/1×1 grids; multi-cell grids byte-unchanged. Verified vs headless FCP: engine tile now renders
at frame centre (was left-edge). golden 2123/0, tsc clean, Objects__Squares 14×8 unchanged (25.74 dB).

POOR-ORACLE FINDING (do NOT re-chase): the aggressive minimizer reduced Objects__Squares to a 1×1
grid. After the centering fix the 1×1 tile is centered but ~2.17× too SMALL (engine 136px vs FCP
295px final). ROOT CAUSE of the residual is that hand-editing Columns/Rows on the 14×8 template is a
POOR ORACLE — FCP special-cases degenerate grids:
  - 1×1 → 1 tile at fixed 295px (final-frame)
  - 2×1 → renders BLACK (nothing)
  - 4×1 → only 2 of 4 tiles render, each 295px
FCP's tile is ALWAYS a fixed 295px regardless of grid; the real 14×8 tiles TESSELLATE correctly
(engine & FCP both mean~121 at t=1, 35.8 dB fully-revealed). So the 1×1 size mismatch is an
over-minimization artifact, NOT a shipped-path bug. The faithful repro is the real 14×8.

PER-TILE REVEAL IS BINARY (re-confirmed by fine-t sweep): a center tile SNAPS 0→98.8 between t=0.175
and t=0.180 (single frame, NO fade). The shipped block model's `front >= v/n ? 1 : 0` binary step is
CORRECT per-tile behavior. The residual 14×8 divergence (16.8 dB at t=0.2-0.6) is purely the reveal
ORDER/permutation (which tile snaps at which t) — the Ozone-core-draw-loop reveal index that is
decoupled from the Particles calcIdx (Spearman −0.07, characterized 2026-07-26, DEAD END without GPU
draw-loop decode). Wavefront col-coverage at t=0.3: FCP has partial cols 4/9 (80/89px) the engine
misses AND engine over-reveals col 2 (503 vs 380) — an order-permutation difference, not softness.

## 2026-07-26 (cont) — Squares tile SIZE decoded (295px) but COUPLED to reveal (can't ship alone)
DECODE (clean, unconfounded — varied cell node Scale id=105 in FCP): FCP tileW_final = vertRange ×
cellScale × nodeScale EXACTLY: ns 0.5→73px, 1.0→142px, 2.11→295px (predicted 69/138/292). tileW is
INDEPENDENT of grid cols/rows/sizeWidth. The engine renders tiles at HALF (137px) because the scene
rasterizes at native canvas W=4096 and api.ts RESAMPLES ÷(4096/1920)=÷2.13 to output, cancelling one
nodeScale=rsx factor. FCP's 295px tiles OVERLAP the ~112px grid spacing 2.6×; the engine's 137px tiles
merely ABUT — so each engine tile covers ¼ the area FCP shows mid-reveal (confirmed: engine "corner
tiles" measured 275px were actually 2 merged 137px tiles).
TRIED THE FIX (multiply tile basis by W/outputRefWidth to survive resample → tile_final =
verts×cellScale×nodeScale). tsc clean, but it REGRESSED Squares 16.75→11.51 dB: with correct 295px
tiles the engine OVER-reveals (t=0.2 mean 45→78 vs FCP 45). REVERTED. Root reason: the shipped block
model (137px tiles + block reveal, 25.74 dB) matches FCP's COVERAGE FRACTION by revealing MORE small
tiles; correct 295px tiles need the correct (FEWER-tile) Ozone reveal SIMULTANEOUSLY — you cannot fix
tile size without the matching reveal order/count. FCP coverage steps in plateaus: 0→14→36→36→52→52
→52→82→82→86→100% (quantized to keyframe times). FCP reveal PATTERN (grid-sampled, overlap-corrupted):
4 corner 2×2 blocks first (t=0.1), then a center 6×4 block (t=0.2), growing symmetrically, gaps last.
This is NOT center-distance (farthest-first gives corners but no simultaneous center) and NOT a diagonal.
The grid-sample is corrupted by 295px tile overlap so the "cells" aren't distinct tile reveals — this is
the SAME Ozone-core-draw-loop reveal-index dead-end (Spearman −0.07 vs Particles calcIdx). CONCLUSION:
tile-size decode is CORRECT and banked; shipping it requires the coupled reveal decode (GPU draw loop),
still a dead-end. Squares stays at 25.74 dB (block model). Do NOT ship tile-size alone.

The committed minimized Stylized__Center (struct_after=6) is a PROVEN POOR ORACLE. In the 6-element

REAL full-transition divergence (11.5-13.9 dB): the LEFT half. Bisected by disabling each panel in
the full motr — culprit is **"Big blue" (id=390117)**. The engine paints it as a flat near-WHITE
panel [233,242,244] (its solid Fill Color 0.914/0.949/0.957 × 255, RAW sRGB) covering cols ~104-1184.
FCP renders that same region as a flat light-CYAN [206,227,231] — consistent with the panel drawn at
~0.88 opacity over the blue Transition-B photo (implied α per channel 0.86/0.92/0.91), OR a different
gradient-stop colour. "Big blue" has BOTH a solid Fill Color (white-ish) AND a Gradient; the engine's
isSolidPanel path (compositor/index.ts ~686) supports ONLY a single panelFill solid colour — NO
gradient, and paints at full opacity. Candidate root causes to decode next: (a) panel should render
its GRADIENT not the solid Fill Color; (b) panelFillOpacity/gradient-opacity ~0.88 not being applied;
(c) panelFill uses RAW sRGB while the general fillColor path uses srgbFillToOutput (linearized) —
inconsistent. NEEDS a faithful re-minimized repro (protect drop-zone Transition A/B + the panel
shapes) to iterate — the current 6-element repro cannot exercise this (renders black).

## 2026-07-26 (cont) — Slide_In gradient: Y-DOWN direction DECODED; brightness needs colourspace decode
DECODED (verified): Motion's Gradient generator (pluginUUID 40091D89) authors Start/End endpoints in
TEXTURE space with Y +DOWN (top-centre origin), NOT the canvas +up convention renderLinearGradient
uses. Flipping to `cy=(y+0.5)-hh` makes BOTH the minimized and FULL Slide_In render light@top→dark@
bottom, matching FCP's trend (was inverted). The generator composites blend=normal, opacity=1, clipped
by ONE rig-selected mask ("...down" variant, Direction=1) over Transition B — so at col960 the visible
colour IS the pure gradient (no B blend), making it a clean colour oracle.
BLOCKER (do NOT ship the Y-flip alone): (a) it REGRESSES the minimized min-PSNR gate (19.2→13.5) — the
PRE-flip inverted gradient accidentally CROSSED FCP's curve, giving lower MSE; the flip is directionally
correct but exposes a brightness gap. (b) Engine gradient is ~80 too BRIGHT. Best model so far
(Y-down + interpolate RAW sRGB stops + srgbToLinear the OUTPUT) matches G/B channels within ~15 but RED
is consistently too HIGH: full col960 row540 FCP=[4,105,132] vs model[60,118,121]; row830 FCP=[0,90,119]
vs [33,89,92]. FCP's RED collapses toward 0 at the dark end faster than any lerp+linearize model — points
to a colourspace/gamma factor (stops carry Gamma 2.2 + Color Space id=11; generator HDR White Level 0.95)
not yet decoded. NEXT: decode whether stops interpolate in linear (loc0l/loc1l) vs sRGB, and how the
per-stop Gamma 2.2 + HDR White Level 0.95 factor in — ship Y-down + the colour decode TOGETHER as one
complete fix (decode-don't-fit; do not ship the partial flip).

## 2026-07-26 (cont) — Slide_In gradient FULLY ROOT-CAUSED (2 bugs; needs generator color-link subsystem)
Isolated the gradient generator cleanly via <enabled>0</enabled> bisection (disable TransA/B + Clone,
render the gradient-ONLY motr through BOTH engines). Definitive gradient-only col960 @ t=0.5:
  FCP:    row300=[22,130,156] → row1050=[0,86,118]  (light→dark DOWN; saturated cyan-blue, low red)
  ENGINE: row300=[106,163,166] → row1050=[190,219,221] (dark→light DOWN INVERTED; low sat, high red)
TWO ROOT-CAUSED BUGS:
1. Y-DIRECTION INVERTED (confirmed): Motion's Gradient generator authors Start/End in Y-DOWN texture
   space. renderLinearGradient uses cy=hh-(y+0.5) (Y-up). Fix is cy=(y+0.5)-hh. (Verified direction;
   do NOT ship alone — regresses min-PSNR gate via accidental curve-crossing, and colour still wrong.)
2. COLOUR-LINK NOT APPLIED to the generator's stops: the .motr has 6 Link behaviors ("Link 1/4
   red/green/blue") whose affectingChannel = "./2/1/310/1/<tagId>/3/<ch>" — i.e. Object>Gradient(id=1)>
   stops(id=310)>RGB(1)>stop(<tagId>)>Color(3)>channel. The parser's parseColorTarget ONLY recognizes

FCP f'≈0.72 (my power=0.565, UNDERSHOOTS). So the power-curve midpoint is right at low-mid t but too

and linear-SPACE interpolation (row300 R=72 vs FCP 22, worse). Current model (sRGB-interp + power
midpoint + linearize-output) is the best of the three. The exact Motion midpoint interpolation near the

## 2026-07-26 (cont) — Slide_In midpoint curve DECODED: f' ≈ t^2 (Interpolation param, not Middle)
Decoded the exact gradient interpolation curve by reversing FCP's implied blend fraction f' (sRGB space)
from the isolated gradient col960 (loc0=[0,158,181], loc1=[255,255,255] linked):
  row0 t=0.806 f'=0.774; row160 t=0.687 f'=0.508; row320 t=0.567 f'=0.308; row640 t=0.328 f'=0.083.
The curve is f' ≈ t^EXPONENT with EXPONENT ≈ 2.0 (t^2 fits err=179; t^1.8 err=182; Middle-derived
t^2.348 err=298; pure linear err=498). So the shipped per-stop MIDDLE power (ln0.5/lnM = 2.348 for
M=0.744) is CLOSE but the true exponent is ~2.0 — which matches the gradient's "Interpolation" param
(id=100, value=2). HYPOTHESIS: Interpolation=2 sets a gamma/power-2 fraction curve, and the Middle
either doesn't apply or applies differently than the standard f^(ln0.5/lnM). NOT yet shipped: need a
SECOND gradient with different Middle+Interpolation to disambiguate (is exponent == Interpolation value?
does Middle still matter?). Interpolation in sRGB space then linearize-output is CONFIRMED (linear-space
interp gives negative f' below mid-frame — impossible). Residual undershoot at the very top (t=0.806:
t^2=0.65 vs FCP 0.774) may be a panel-edge/second-element effect. Current shipped Middle-power is a
reasonable approximation (Slide_In already 8.34→21.16 dB); refining to Interpolation-power-2 is a small
gain (err 298→179) but needs the enum semantics decoded first (decode-don't-fit). DEFER pending a 2nd
gradient sample to confirm Interpolation→exponent mapping.

## 2026-07-26 (cont) — Light Sweep TIMELINE-COMPRESSION fixed (animationEndSec 1.301→1.433)
ROOT CAUSE of the f12 "over-bright" divergence was a TIMELINE MISALIGNMENT, not a rendering bug: the
engine's animationEndSec was 1.301s (last spatial keyframe) but the authored scene span is 1.433s
(<duration>43</duration>/30fps). The s24/min-score harness feeds NORMALIZED t and each engine multiplies
by its own end — FCP-headless by the span (1.433), the TS engine by animationEndSec (1.301) — so at the
same normalized t the two rendered DIFFERENT scene times (~0.07s apart), mis-aligning every frame.
The B-hold fix (731f517) should have caught it but required B.out<=span; Light Sweep's B.out=1.47 >
span 1.433 (padded editor timeline) so it was excluded. FIX (661981d): gate the B-hold on its START
within span (inS<span) and CLAMP out to span (held=min(outS,span)). animationEndSec 1.301→1.433 → tail
now settles on B matching FCP (f23 center [204,196,190] == FCP [203,196,189], 23.7 dB). Center unchanged.
RESIDUAL (now correctly aligned): mid-transition (f10-f18, t≈0.6-1.07) the engine is TOO BRIGHT (f18
en 203 vs FCP 146) — the lens-flare .mov screen-overlay and/or softLight gradient generators over-
brighten. Worst ~9.8 dB. Separate follow-up (needs the flare fade / screen-blend / softLight-gradient
decode). NOTE the minimized Stylized__Light_Sweep case (struct 160→2, 6.92 dB) is likely OVER-reduced

## 2026-07-26 (cont) — Smear refined: matches at f0, diverges as B enters (A→B order/timing)
Refined the Movements__Smear diagnosis. At f0 (t=0) engine==FCP (43 dB, both show the smeared-A gradient
cols [48,103,107,117]). FCP HOLDS that smeared-A gradient (mean 97.3) through f0-f2, then transitions.
The ENGINE diverges immediately at f1 (t=0.017): engine jumps to 119.6 while FCP stays 97.3. B's drop-
zone in=0.033 (f2), so at f1 B shouldn't contribute yet — but the engine's frame changes. By f10 FCP
shows pure B (mean 121, B fully on top) while the engine shows smeared-A over B (A first-in-list=top,
opacity const 1.0). So TWO coupled issues: (1) the engine's A→B compositing ORDER — A (smeared, opaque,
top) covers B, but FCP ends with B on top (the incoming clip takes over); (2) timing — the engine's
smear/B state changes at f1 while FCP holds A through ~f2. Both are the drop-zone A→B crossfade/order
semantics (same class as Center/Light_Sweep). The Scrape filter math + the f0 match confirm the FILTER

FINAL Smear mechanism: the "A" plate (transitionA) carries a retimeValue {type1, keyframes t=0→frame1,

=1 transitionA plate's retimed frame passes its content end, it should go TRANSPARENT (not persist) so

## 2026-07-26 (SYSTEMIC) — Drop-zone A→B handoff is a SHARED subsystem (Center/Light_Sweep/Smear)
Smear's wrapToA=false (no filled-shape overlay), holdIncomingB/filterRevealSettleB=true. So A is NOT
force-persisted by wrapToA — A is simply visible by NORMAL timing (in=-0.767,out=0.434, opacity const
1.0) and rendered on top of B, covering it. At f0 (B not yet in, in=0.033) engine==FCP (both smeared-A).
Once B enters (~f2), FCP DROPS A (fully transparent at f10) and shows B; the engine keeps A opaque over
B. A's retimeValue (frames 1→38, extrapolation=1) advances (frame 24 at f0, 29 at f10) but there's NO
hard frame-count cutoff (A is visible at frame 24 in f0). So the A-drop is the A→B CROSSFADE, not a
frame-exhaustion. THREE transitions now share this exact root cause:
  • Center: A (out=2.40)→B chain, engine held A on the tail (partly addressed by B-hold animationEndSec).
  • Light_Sweep: B fades in from the flash; engine shows B early/over-bright (timeline fix helped).
  • Smear: A(smeared) should hand off to B once B enters; engine keeps A opaque over B.
COMMON MECHANISM: FCP crossfades an OUTGOING transitionA plate to the INCOMING transitionB drop-zone
driven by their overlapping timing windows + retimeValue extrapolation — the outgoing A fades to
transparent as B takes over. The engine lacks this A→B crossfade: it composites both by static timing
windows + z-order, so the outgoing A persists opaque over the incoming B. HIGHEST-VALUE NEXT SUBSYSTEM:
decode FCP's drop-zone A→B crossfade law (how the outgoing plate's opacity ramps to 0 as the incoming
plate's window opens — likely a linear crossfade over the [B.in, A.out] overlap, or driven by the
retimeValue frame position). A single correct crossfade model would lift Center + Smear (and help
Light_Sweep). This is the shared thread behind the drop-zone-timing family. Bounded, high-ROI.

## 2026-07-26 (cont) — Smear A-drop PINNED: A hard-drops transparent at t≈0.078s (retime/media-end on 1-frame clip)
Fine-grained FCP A-plate-alone (B disabled) alpha trajectory: opaque 254 through t=0.067, then a FAST
ramp 253→239→225→205 over t=0.067-0.076, then SNAP to alpha 0 at t=0.079+. So A disappears at t≈0.078s
— FAR earlier than its raw timing out=0.434s. KEY: the "Drop Zone Transition A" CLIP has
missingDuration=0.0333s = exactly ONE frame @30fps. A's retimeValue (frames 1→38 over 1.234s,
extrapolation=1) advances the clip-frame; the 1-frame clip's media is exhausted almost immediately, so
FCP drops the plate to transparent at ~t=0.078 (retimed frame ≈3.3 scene / ≈26 clip). The engine holds A
opaque to its raw out=0.434 (ignoring that the retimed 1-frame clip ran out of media). So the fix:
a transitionA/drop-zone image with a retimeValue advancing past its CLIP's frame count (missingDuration
·fps frames) must go TRANSPARENT at that point, not persist to the raw timing `out`. The engine's
visibility uses raw [in,out]; it must additionally clip on retimed-frame > clip-frame-count. NEXT: read
the clip's frame count (missingDuration·fps) + map retimeValue frame at t, drop the plate when
frame>count. Bounded retime-subsystem fix, boundary pinned to t≈0.078. (Same retime family as the
Center/Light_Sweep A→B handoff — this is the concrete decode of the "outgoing A drops" half.)
golden 2123/0, tsc clean, working tree clean.

## 2026-07-26 (cont) — Smear A clip = 1-frame still (out at frame 0); retime-frame indexing is the last decode

## 2026-07-26 (cont) — Smear CORRECTED root cause: Crop=1 Scrape produces a TRANSPARENT WIPE, not opaque fold
Finer spatial analysis overturns the pure-timing hypothesis. FCP's A-plate-alone is a WIPE, not a
uniform fade: at t=0.076 alpha is left=0 (transparent), center=255, right=255 — A is progressively
WIPED to TRANSPARENT from the left, fully gone by t=0.079. The engine's decoded Scrape instead does an
opaque FOLD (x≥threshold → sample col-65, fully opaque) and never produces transparency. So the real
divergence is the SCRAPE FILTER's Crop=1 behavior: with Crop=1 (the shipping default; Movements/Smear
authors Crop=1) the smeared region is CROPPED to TRANSPARENT (a wipe), NOT folded to a column. The
decoded HgcScrape shader in scrape.ts models the FOLD (likely the Crop=0 variant) with NO transparency
path — that's why the engine holds opaque A over B while FCP wipes A away to reveal B. Also FCP wipes
the LOW-d (left) side to transparent while the engine's fold affects the HIGH-d (right) side — so the
Crop transparency is on the d<threshold OR moving-center side. CORRECTED FIX LOCUS: decode the Crop=1
Scrape variant — when Crop enabled, pixels in the crop region output TRANSPARENT (alpha 0) instead of
the col-fold, producing the progressive wipe. This subsumes the earlier "A-plate retime drop" finding
(the wipe IS the A→B reveal; A goes transparent via Crop, not via a timing drop). NEXT: re-examine
-[PAEScrape canThrowRenderOutput] + HgcScrape for the Crop=1 branch (the shader RE captured only the
fold; Crop's transparency path was noted "DEAD branch" but Crop=1 clearly changes output to a wipe).
Bounded filter-decode task. golden 2123/0, tsc clean, working tree clean.

## 2026-07-26 (DECODED) — Smear root cause = Crop=1 applies a POST-WARP crop-to-transparent pass (engine omits it)
Disassembled -[PAEScrape canThrowRenderOutput] @0x5d930. The flow: get Center(parm1)→pixels, Rotation
(parm2, fneg'd), Amount(parm3), Crop(parm4, getIntValue). Build the HgcScrape warp node (the fold shader
in scrape.ts — that part is correct). THEN at 0x5dc00: `ldr w8,[sp,#0x14]; cbz w8, skip` tests the CROP
toggle — if Crop!=0 (Movements/Smear has Crop=1), it calls **crop:fromImage:toImage: @0x5dc18**, a
SEPARATE post-warp crop pass. This crop is what produces the TRANSPARENT WIPE (A wiped from the left to
alpha 0 by t=0.078). The engine implements ONLY the HgcScrape warp (fold to col-65, opaque) and OMITS
the Crop=1 crop pass → holds opaque A over B instead of wiping A transparent to reveal B.
ROOT CAUSE (final, corrects all prior Smear hypotheses — not order, not retime-drop, not scale): the
Scrape filter's Crop=1 post-warp crop-to-transparent pass is MISSING in the engine. FIX: after the
scrape warp, when Crop=1, apply the crop (make the region outside the valid-source rect transparent),
reproducing the wipe. REMAINING DECODE: the crop RECT — what region survives vs goes transparent (from
Center/Rotation/Amount + the warp's valid-source extent). Likely crops to where the inverse-map texcoord
stayed in [0,1] (i.e. make out-of-source-bounds pixels transparent — the engine's sample() already
returns transparent for out-of-[0,1], but the FOLD keeps them in-bounds at col-65; the crop pass instead
marks the folded/smeared region transparent). NEXT: decode crop:fromImage:toImage: args (the crop rect
computation) to reproduce the exact wipe boundary. Bounded filter-pass addition; mechanism now decoded.
golden 2123/0, tsc clean, working tree clean.

The engine's warp folds opaque (col-65) and never crops, so A stays opaque over B. REMAINING (bounded):

## 2026-07-26 (cont) — Smear wipe is NEAR-INSTANT: A hard-cuts transparent ~t=0.075-0.079 (sub-frame sweep)
Measured the wipe boundary velocity: leftmost-opaque col = 0 (full) through t=0.067, →373 at t=0.076,

to find where the boundary (center + threshold along +X axis) exits col 1920 → that's the transparent

## 2026-07-26 (cont) — Smear crop boundary ≈ center_px − threshold (at the 1 partial frame); near-instant otherwise
Decoded the animated Center X (Scrape param, curveTime=scene−(−0.767)): sweeps X_rel 1.043(t0)→0.345

Crop wipe as A going transparent at the trigger frame (the frame where the crop boundary sweeps the

## 2026-07-26 — CRITICAL minimizer-reliability finding (persistent-worker state drift)
A Squares sub-agent found the persistent-worker scorer can produce FALSE-LOW PSNRs: engine/GPU/
context state drifts between renders in a long-lived worker, so two renders of the SAME doc can
differ. This makes the minimizer ACCEPT invalid removals (it stripped Squares down to a NON-
animating static doc that a clean per-frame oracle scores 43.56 dB "ok" — no divergence — but the
drifting worker reported false-low PSNR). LESSON: any persistent-worker speedup MUST be
determinism-checked (same .motr rendered twice == identical) before trusting its scores. Clean
per-frame oracle (fresh subprocess/frame, fct/score.py) is the reliable reference.
Squares real bug (unchanged): replicator shuffle-ORDER reveal edge-symmetry (replicator.ts
shuffledSequenceOrder, FULLY DECODED). Residual ~16 dB is engine-vs-GUI (headless PRNG != GUI PRNG,
corr 0.102) — BLOCKED on a Squares GUI ground-truth capture; do not fit engine to headless order.

## 2026-07-26 — SHIPPED: parser resolves factory TYPE by UUID (not <description>) — commit 45c55e8
Re-minimizing Stylized__Center (fast tool: struct 123->3, 54 lines) isolated a single "Right full"
Shape (factoryID 14, uuid 712462a4...) that FCP fills WHITE but the engine rendered BLACK. Root cause:
the parser built its factoryID->type map from the <description> TEXT only; the (new, faster) minimizer's
attribute/generic passes stripped <description>Shape</description> from the <factory> shell, so
factories.get(14)='' -> node defaulted to 'group' -> not rendered. FCP-headless was unaffected because
FCP identifies a factory by its stable UUID, not the description text.
FIX: added FACTORY_UUID_TYPE (57 UUIDs, unambiguous across all 65 templates); parser resolves type by
UUID first, falls back to <description> for unknown UUIDs. Now Shape/Replicator/etc. classify robustly
regardless of a missing/blank <description>. Verified: node 354582 parses type=shape/fillColor=white/
isSolidPanel=true (was group); tsc clean; golden 2123/0.
This is a GENUINE engine robustness fix (matches FCP's UUID-keyed resolution), but Center is NOT fully
fixed — the shape now RENDERS but still diverges (f16 ~8 dB: engine mean 192.6 vs FCP 158.4; the score
command's "MEAN 99.0" is a stale/mismatched-frame artifact, NOT real — direct per-frame PSNR shows the
residual). Re-minimizing again with shapes parsing correctly to isolate the REAL remaining divergence
(fill/gradient/panel-choreography). LESSON: the faster minimizer's aggressive attribute/description
stripping can expose engine fragilities FCP doesn't share (description-vs-UUID) — those ARE real bugs to
fix (RULE 1), and fixing them hardens the engine even when the specific minimized repro was degenerate.

## 2026-07-26 — SHIPPED: parser resolves factory TYPE by UUID (not <description>) [45c55e8]
ROOT CAUSE (found via re-minimized Stylized__Center f16): the parser mapped factoryID->type
using the <factory> <description> TEXT. FCP identifies a factory by its stable <uuid>. So any
scenenode whose <description> was missing/blank silently degraded to a generic 'group' and
rendered NOTHING. Surfaced because the (improved) minimizer's boilerplate/generic passes strip
<description> from <factory> shells — FCP-headless (UUID-keyed) still rendered the shape white,
our engine (description-keyed) rendered black. Stylized__Center's "Right full" Shape (factory 14,
uuid 712462a4...) parsed as group -> black.
FIX: FACTORY_UUID_TYPE table (57 UUIDs, unambiguous, harvested from all 65 shipped Transition
templates); factories map resolves UUID first, falls back to <description> for unknown UUIDs.
Now node 354582 parses type=shape, fillColor=white, isSolidPanel=true (was group). tsc clean;
golden 2123/0. This is the FCP-faithful mechanism (decode-don't-fit) AND it hardens the minimizer
against a whole false-divergence class (stripped factory descriptions).
STATUS: real improvement, NOT a complete Center fix — direct frame compare still shows f16 ~8.2 dB
(engine mean 192.6 vs FCP 158.4): the shape now renders but the panel choreography/fill still
diverges. Re-minimizing Center with shapes classifying correctly to isolate the residual.
NOTE: `score` MEAN 99.0 was a stale/summary artifact — trust the per-frame compare, not that number.

## 2026-07-26 (cont) — Center degenerate-repro decode: vertex values are INERT (needs non-stripped repro)
Probed the _t_center3 minimized repro (single Shape, factory 14) directly through FCP-headless with
controlled input variation. FINDINGS: FCP renders a FIXED white polygon (left edge const col 749,
top full-width [749..1170] rows 0..540, right edge slopes to bottom-left) that is INERT to the
surviving vertex values — setting the one X vertex (385) or the Y vertices (388/384) to ±900 changes
NOTHING; adding a Position X=592 changes nothing; only EMPTYING curve_X entirely removes the shape.
So the polygon is NOT driven by the stripped vertex coords — FCP is rendering a DEFAULT/animation-pose
fallback (id=117 "Animation" curve, whose keyframes+index were stripped). CONCLUSION: this is a
genuinely degenerate repro whose DRIVING parameters were removed by the minimizer's value/generic
passes — decoding "match FCP" here would mean fitting FCP's default-shape fallback on inert inputs,
which is guessing (decode-don't-fit forbids). The FULL Center still diverges 8.15 dB at f16 (engine
mean 192.6 vs FCP 158.4 — engine now OVER-bright after UUID fix made more shapes render) — that is the
REAL bug. `score` MEAN 99.0 is a STALE reading (does not re-render); direct per-frame compare is truth.
NEXT: the minimizer over-strips shape-driving params (vertex Value curves, Fill) that FCP tolerates but
that carry the real geometry — need a re-minimize that stops before the shape goes inert, OR diagnose
the over-bright on the full scene (which shape/panel now over-covers).

## 2026-07-26 (cont) — Center degenerate Shape: FCP output is INPUT-INERT (decode blocked, not skipped)
Exhaustive FCP-headless probes on _t_center3 (single Shape, factory 14, only id=117 curve_X[1 vert]/
curve_Y[2 verts] survive; Fill/Crop/Position/index=/default= all stripped):
 - FCP renders a FIXED white polygon: left edge const col 749, top [749..1170] rows 0..540, right edge
   slopes (1170,540)->(749,1079). Static across all frames.
 - Setting the surviving vertex VALUES (385=-383.9, 388=-571.4, 384=+571.4) to ±900 changes NOTHING.
 - Setting empty vertices to 0 gives a DIFFERENT (diamond) shape, so empty!=0.
 - Emptying curve_X entirely -> shape vanishes (so the X vertex COUNT matters, its VALUE doesn't).
 - Injected Position X=592 doesn't move it.
CONCLUSION: for this degenerate shape (stripped of Fill/Crop/index=/default=), FCP's rendered polygon
does NOT derive from any surviving input — it's a canonical default-shape fallback driven by params the
minimizer DESTROYED (vertex index= ordering + default= coords). There is no input->output law to decode
from the reduced doc (output is constant w.r.t. every surviving knob), so "match FCP here" would require
FITTING FCP's internal default-shape geometry on inert inputs = guessing (decode-don't-fit forbids).
The ENGINE bug is real & general though: extractAxisVertices DROPS vertices lacking a folder/value and
parseShape truncates X/Y to min(len) — so it degenerates to <1 usable vertex and renders nothing, where
FCP keeps all control points. FIX DIRECTION (needs a NON-inert repro to verify): keep all vertices,
fall back value->default->0, pair X/Y by index (pad short axis), don't truncate. Deferred until a repro
whose output actually responds to the vertex inputs (the minimizer over-stripped this one past the point
of carrying signal). REAL full-Center divergence persists: f16 8.15 dB (engine 192.6 vs FCP 158.4,
engine over-bright) — `score` MEAN 99.0 is a STALE artifact, direct per-frame compare is truth.

Fitted FCP's rendered half-width vs the one real vertex |X385| (white fill injected, ozengine probe):
   X385=-100 -> halfw 54.5 ; -300 -> 164.5 ; -500 -> 274.5 ; -800 -> 439.5 ; -960 -> 527.5

and Y is NOT scaled (full frame height, only frame-clipped). The engine instead fills the raw one-sided

## 2026-07-26 (cont) — Shape rasterizer: 0.55 factor PINNED, but it's WINDING not a scale (needs OZ disasm)
Pinned the X-extent factor by linear fit over X385 sweep: halfw = 0.55000*|X385| - 0.5 (the -0.5 is
half-pixel AA). So factor = EXACTLY 11/20 = 0.55, symmetric ±, Y unaffected (full height) for the
baseline vertex config. BUT the Y-coupling probe breaks the "simple scale" model: changing the two Y
vertices from ±571 to ±200 changes the render to x=(-960,-1) — a totally different one-sided fill.
So the symmetric ±0.55*|X385| is NOT a coordinate scale; it emerges from the FILL WINDING of this
specific self-intersecting polygon (verts (0,0),(0,-Yb),(Xv,0),(0,+Yt) with the empties=0). FCP's
scanline fill of this bowtie differs from the engine's even-odd fill (compositor/shapes.ts uses
even-odd, line 18/428). The engine fills the raw one-sided hull [-384..0]; FCP's winding yields the
symmetric ±211. CONCLUSION: this is a polygon FILL-RULE / self-intersection winding difference, decodable
only by reading the OZ shape rasterizer (OZRotoshapeRender / OZMoShapeVerticesController) — the exact
even-odd-vs-nonzero + vertex-closing behavior. NOT guessing a winding swap (even-odd->nonzero didn't
match in a quick raster check either). DEFERRED to OZ rasterizer disasm. The vertex-PARSE half is done
+ shipped (048d4ec); this is the geometry half. Higher-value: this degenerate self-intersecting case is
rare — the vertex-parse fix (shipped) is the broadly-useful part. Next loop: pick a fresh worst case.

## 2026-07-26 (cont12) — DECISIVE: absent vertex Value ≠ 0 (but well-formed shapes MATCH; degenerate-only)
Ran the clean discriminating probe on the ACTUAL _t_center_ss file (FCP-headless f16, white fill injected):
   empties ABSENT (as-shipped)  -> FCP fills RECTANGLE x[-2..385] uniform (top half)
   empties -> explicit "0"      -> FCP fills TRIANGLE {0:(-2,22),180:(-2,143),360:(-2,264),540:(0,384)}
These are DIFFERENT FCP renders. So FCP does NOT treat an absent vertex Value as coordinate 0. This
DISPROVES the premise of commit 048d4ec ("empty vertex value = 0"): 048d4ec's "byte-for-byte" proof held
only for the OLD _t_center3 config where absent and 0 happened to coincide (confirmed: the diamond probe
(0,-200)(-200,0)(v2)(0,200) gives IDENTICAL renders for v2-absent and v2=0). The engine (value=0) makes
the TRIANGLE; FCP-absent makes the RECTANGLE → the _t_center_ss 8.18 dB divergence.

CRUCIAL SCOPING (RULE 1 triage): a well-formed NON-collinear quad MATCHES exactly — CCW square ±250:
FCP x[-252..251] == ENGINE x[-250..249] (1-2px AA). So the engine's shape rasterizer is CORRECT for real
shapes; the divergence is ONLY in the DEGENERATE freeform case (mostly-absent vertices → not enough valued
points to close a polygon), where FCP falls back to a BOUNDING-RECTANGLE of the valued points (x[0..+384]
from the one X value +384; top-half from the one Y value ±571) while the engine builds a value=0 polygon.

NOT SHIPPING a guess: tested drop (continue) → renders NOTHING (worse, the original 048d4ec bug); tested
value=0 → triangle; FCP → rectangle. The exact absent-vertex→bounding-rect fallback rule is not fully
decoded (needs OZChannelCurve::getEnabledVerticesList + the underspecified-shape path disasm). 048d4ec's
value=0 is kept (strictly better than black; correct wherever absent≡0, e.g. the diamond + all well-formed
shapes). This degenerate repro is a value-simplification artifact of the minimizer; the REAL Center shapes
are well-formed rectangles (Shape Type 1) that the engine already rasterizes correctly in geometry —
the REAL Center residual (13.50 dB) is the Type-1 rectangle-vs-polygon-fill + card compositing, NOT this
absent-vertex degenerate. Next real lever: implement Shape Type 1 (bbox rectangle) in the engine.

## 2026-07-26 (cont18) — SHIPPED held-visible-layer timing fix (264b696): _t_center_faithful 8.48 -> 62.12 dB
The reference-node-protecting minimizer (dd09dae) produced a FAITHFUL Center repro (_t_center_faithful:
a held white-fill Shape id 390117 + a Clone + an Image Mask, full sceneSettings incl frameRate). It
exposed TWO real, coupled engine bugs — both now DECODED + SHIPPED (264b696), golden 2123/0:

1. animationEndSec TRUNCATION (parser/index.ts): the keyframe-walk maxT landed at 0.534s (a leftover
   keyframe curve), but Shape 390117 has NO keyframes and a <timing in=1.368s out=3.604s> window. Trimming
   animationEndSec to maxT compressed the whole transition into [0,0.534s]; the progress->time map never
   sampled the shape's window -> f7-f16 rendered BLACK while FCP (playing the authored 5.3s span) shows
   the shape (gray ~36). FIX: a HELD-VISIBLE-LAYER pass (generalizes the Transition-B hold) — when a held
   drawable (non-mask fill Shape / Clone / A|B image) has a <timing> window that OPENS AT/AFTER the
   maxT-trimmed end (in >= animationEndSec) within the span, restore animationEndSec to the AUTHORED scene
   duration (duration/frameRate) so the full span plays like FCP. (Extending only to the layer's own `out`
   3.604s still mis-mapped straddling frames — verified; the full span 5.3s is required.) Gated on
   in >= animationEndSec so keyframe-driven / normal slugs are untouched.
2. isLayerVisible IGNORED `out` for ALL fillColor shapes (evaluator/index.ts): the flash-overlay rule
   (ignore the too-early `out`, let the opacity fade tail decide) fired for EVERY non-mask fillColor shape,
   so Shape 390117 (no opacity curve) held through the tail (f17-f23) where FCP drops it to BLACK at
   out=3.604s. FIX: only ignore `out` when the shape's opacity is an ANIMATED Curve (the genuine flash
   fade); a static/no-opacity filled shape honors its timing `out` as a hard cutoff. Lights/Flash's
   animated-opacity flash rectangles keep their fade tail (golden green).

## 2026-07-26 (cont20) — degenerate-vertex polygon: FCP corner (v3.X, v0.Y) confirmed; index pairings DISPROVEN
Decoded FCP's exact filled triangle for shape 390117 (verts index (0,+597),(0,0),(0,-597),(-546,0)):

  (0,+597),(-546,0),(0,-597) — apex (-546) at MID-Y (0) — the index-paired polygon.

## 2026-07-26 (cont21) — DECODED via controlled FCP probe: shape vertices are Y-DOWN (engine uses Y-UP, backwards)
_t_center_v2 (re-minimized WITH vertex indices preserved by the 872c128 fix) still diverges 8.10 dB, so the

   (TOP). => FCP maps vertex Y=+400 to the BOTTOM => FCP uses pixel_row = H/2 + vy (Y-DOWN, +Y=down). The
   engine's rasterizeShape toPixel uses `H/2 - vy` (Y-UP). THE ENGINE'S SHAPE-VERTEX Y IS FLIPPED THE WRONG
   WAY vs FCP. Verified this single rule (yc=-vy) reproduces v2's FCP triangle exactly (0,0),(+546,0),(0,+597.6).
   Golden passes today only because real/golden fill shapes are Y-SYMMETRIC rectangles (Center's real shape

   3 of 4 verts, creating the first ASYMMETRIC shape that exposes the latent convention bug.
   BLAST RADIUS CONCERN: rasterizeShape.toPixel is shared by FILL and MASK paths (masks.ts calls the same
   fn). Negating vy flips BOTH. Must measure the full scored-suite delta before shipping (golden can't catch
   it — all golden shapes symmetric). NOT yet shipped pending blast-radius measurement.
2. EMPTY-SLOT HANDLING (separate, NOT decoded): _t_center_faithful's shape (X=[0,0,0,-546] Y=[+597,0,-597,0])
   under the correct yc=-vy still gives a mid-left DIAMOND, but FCP renders a SLANT with corner (-546,+597).

   FCP; hold-both => empty render. Undecoded — needs OZChannelCurve::getVertexValue empty-slot semantics.

suite with a toPixel yc=-vy flip to confirm it's globally-correct (net-positive) and not a fit; if net-positive
ship it, else scope to the exact path. This is a candidate SYSTEMIC fix (shape-vertex Y) touching many slugs.

## 2026-07-26 (cont28) — computeFraming geometry DECODED (sub-agent + probes), impl pending projection decode
Sub-agent decoded OZScene::computeFraming @0x6ca18 from the ARM64 disassembly + I confirmed with controlled
headless-FCP probes on _t_clonespin3:
  - NO tan(AOV/2) anywhere; the framing DISTANCE = max over axes of 500·(halfExtProj/halfFrame − 1), where
    halfExtProj is HALF the target bbox extent AFTER projection to the render frame, halfFrame = frame/2, and
    500 is a fixed reference-depth constant (@0x6ce68 mov/movk → 0x407f400000000000 = 500.0). max-of-axes via
    fcsel @0x6ce9c; guard |d|<1e-7→1.0 (eps 0x3e7ad7f29abcaf48).
  - The camera targets the PROJECTED BBOX CENTER (0.5·(min+max), @0x6cec4-0x6cee4) unprojected to world
    (@0x6d03c [x22+0x500]) → the target lands at FRAME CENTER. NO local-axis Path Offset shift of the framing
    point (the TS offZ=pathOffset.z·apex has no counterpart; Path Offset is a projected nudge only).
  - Framing mode (id=203) / Orientation (id=208) are NOT branched inside computeFraming — consumed upstream in
    target selection; the only in-function branch is a LiCamera capability bool [x20+0x278]==1 (frame-all vs
    single-target).
PROBES (headless FCP on _t_clonespin3, all at f12) — the framed plate is 256x256 CENTERED (959,539) and
INVARIANT to: Path Offset (default/X=Y=0/Z=2000 all 256x256 centered), AOV (30/60/90 all 256x256), and plate
Scale (50/100/200% all 256x256). => FCP normalizes the target to a FIXED on-screen size, centered — exactly
the projected-ratio·500 dolly (dollies back proportionally as the target scales, keeping projected size fixed;

ENGINE currently renders 624x350 OFF-CENTER (818,444) vs FCP 256x256 centered (960,540) — wrong because
framePose uses world-space max(hw,hh)/tan(AOV/2) + local-axis Path Offset. 
IMPL PENDING: rewriting framePose to project bbox → distance=max(500·(halfExtProj/halfFrame−1)) → unproject
projected-center → look-at needs the exact projection matrix behind [x22+0x568] (static .motr camera vs
identity-ortho vs renderState) — undecoded. Shipping the projected-ratio law without that risks a fit. The
framing TOPOLOGY is already correct (constant pose, _t_clonespin3 worst 7.41->16.61); the size/centering is the
bounded remaining delta. DEFERRED behind decoding [x22+0x568]/[x22+0x500]. Next actionable: Stylized__Lower.

## PAELevels White In=0 degenerate — RESOLVED (2026-07-26) [was a "blocker", RULE 1 win]
The "White In=0 minimizer artifact -> do NOT fit" blocker is RETIRED. Per RULE 1 it was a REAL
engine sign bug, not an artifact. DECODED via 50 REAL-FCP PAELevels transfer probes (transfer_batch,
uniform-color inputs sweeping whiteIn + degenerate/inverted blackIn,whiteIn):
  FCP HgcLevels stage-1 denominator = (whiteIn-blackIn) - 1e-5   (shader ((inBlack-inWhite)+1e-5))
  NOT the engine's `range || 0.001`. Degenerate/inverted range -> NEGATIVE slope -> BLACK (FCP ~0-13);
  old positive 0.001 -> WHITE blowup (227). Reproduces all 50 points to 0 error incl. blackIn=whiteIn=0.5
  -> [in16:255,in96:0]. Byte-identical on all non-degenerate legs (within 1 code).
FIX 3be123a: both LUT sites (buildLevelsWSLUT + levelsFilter) use `range - 1e-5`.
VERIFY: _t_upover2 1.01 -> 22.42 dB; test:node 2123/0 (all PAELevels legs green); tsc clean.
Probe scripts: /tmp/probe_whitein.py, /tmp/probe_deg.py (both use fct/parity/transfer_batch.py).

## PAELevels White In=0 degenerate — FIXED & UNBLOCKED (2026-07-26)
Retired the "PAELevels White In=0 = minimizer artifact, do not fit" blocker. Per RULE 1 it was
a REAL engine sign bug, decoded cleanly (RULE 2.0 option c) from 50 REAL-FCP transfer probes.
ROOT CAUSE: engine stage-1 input denominator was `(whiteIn-blackIn) || 0.001` (POSITIVE fallback)
→ degenerate range blew up to WHITE (227). FCP's HgcLevels uses `(whiteIn-blackIn) - 1e-5` (the
shader ((inBlack-inWhite)+1e-5) epsilon) → degenerate/inverted range = huge NEGATIVE slope → BLACK.
FIX (3be123a): both LUT sites use `range - 1e-5`. Reproduces ALL 50 probe points to 0 error incl.
the subtle blackIn=whiteIn=0.5 → [in16:255,in96:0]; byte-identical to old on all non-degenerate legs
(within 1 code). _t_upover2 minimized: 1.01 → 22.42 dB. Golden 2123/0. PAELevels stays VERIFIED.
Probe scripts: /tmp/probe_whitein.py, /tmp/probe_deg.py (transfer_batch, uuid 2B221FA1...).

    at ANY center-scale maxes corr 0.18. Transfer engine→FCP is NON-MONOTONIC (eng140→FCP3) ⇒ pixels are
    NOT spatially aligned ⇒ FCP applies a scale/crop/pan to bg6 that the engine's conform does not.

/ Light_Sweep). NOT a Levels bug (that was _t_upover2's White-In, already fixed +21dB). The engine's

Object id=2 drop-zone block) in the minimizer so re-mins stay faithful; then the 3D perspective isolates

## _t_3dr_v4 (10.76dB) — Position-Z perspective: SIGN INVERTED + camera distance ≈2330 (2026-07-26)
Faithful re-minimize (Type-binding now protected) → Image node = Transition-B drop zone with
Position Z curve = −600. FCP perspective-SHRINKS the plate (recedes); engine renders it FLAT full-frame.
FIXED HALF (cb9ed49): parser now extracts Position Z (axis() id-fallback) → m14=−600 reaches the matrix.
REMAINING (projection): the engine's projectPoint uses scale = camZ/(camZ + wz) with camZ=2000, so a
NEGATIVE Position Z (−600) → scale 2000/1400 = 1.43 (ENLARGES, overflows to full-frame). FCP SHRINKS it.
DECODED via controlled FCP Z-probe (Z ∈ {−600,−300,0,+300,+600} through _headless-frame, scale vs Z=0):
    Z=−300 → 0.8865   Z=−600 → 0.7948   (Z≥0 clamps at the source's full-frame cap)
  Fit scale = d/(d − Z_motion): d = 2343 (from Z−300) and 2324 (from Z−600) — CONSISTENT d ≈ 2330.
  So FCP: Motion +Z = TOWARD camera (larger), −Z = away (smaller); camera-less default distance ≈ 2330
  (≈ (1080/2)/tan(26°/2), i.e. a ~26° default AOV — needs confirmation it's not frame-width/other based).
ENGINE BUGS: (1) Position-Z sign inverted vs FCP (engine denom camZ+wz enlarges on −Z); (2) default
camera distance 2000 vs FCP ≈2330. CANDIDATE FIX: negate posZ into m14 (Motion −600 → world +600 →
recedes under the existing camZ+wz formula) AND set the camera-less default distance to ≈2330 — BUT this
touches the SHARED projectPoint/matrix used by rotation-perspective cases (Fall rotX, Reflection anchorZ
hinge, PAEFlop page-flip) that are "verified against GT". MUST verify those don't regress before shipping
(negating only the posZ TRANSLATION, not rotation-induced wz, is the safer scoping). DEFER pending that
regression check across the 3D-transform family. Probe dir /tmp/zprobe. Repro fct/minimized/_t_3dr_v4.

## Perspective-Z / camera-less projection — DECODED, fix DEFERRED (conflict w/ orthographic wins) (2026-07-26)
Full spec: fct/PERSPECTIVE_Z_DECODE.md. Sub-agent decoded FCP's projection from 5 Position-Z + rotX/rotY
headless probes on _t_3dr_v4 (minimized 3D_Rectangle, camera-less, Position Z):
  • FORMULA: scale = d/(d − Z_world), +Z_world=toward viewer. Engine's projectPoint d/(d+wz) is correct
    once fed wz=−Z_world.
  • CAMERA DISTANCE (camera-less default): d = (frameWIDTH/2)/tan(AOV/2), AOV=45° (Motion default) → 2317.6
    for 1920w. 5-point fit d=2324.8±7 (≡44.88°). Scales with WIDTH (1080w probe → d≈1304), NOT height.
    Probe scales: Z-150→0.940, -300→0.886, -450→0.838, -600→0.795, -900→0.721 (all ±0.001 to d/(d−Z)).
  • rotX: engine's existing -rotX negation MATCHES FCP exactly (top width 1598 @60°, d=2318). d=2000→2318
    makes rotX MORE accurate (was 1556). rotY: engine right-near vs FCP left-near — LATENTLY inverted but
    NOT exercised by any passing slug (page-flip uses renderPageFlip's own projection, not projectQuad).
  • BLOCKER: _t_3dr_v4 renders flat because rctx.cameraZ=Infinity — the camera-less scene hits the
    ORTHOGRAPHIC branch (evaluator resolveCamera L1362 distance=Infinity), decoded from Ozone binary
    (OZScene::getActiveCamera null → viewIsOrthographic) AND a MEASURED win for Fall/Color_Planes(+3.0)/
    Reflection(+0.91). So changing distance/sign does NOT move 10.76 dB. FCP DOES perspective-shrink
    camera-less Z-translation → the engine's orthographic decode is OVER-BROAD, but narrowing it regresses
    the tuned rotation slugs. Reconciling needs the separate reason Fall prefers weak foreshortening
    (rotationY sign OR DoF) decoded first, gated across the whole camera-less family — outside verify-only
    scope. DEFERRED as a camera-less-projection subsystem. Probes preserved /tmp/zprobe/.

## 3D_Rectangle camera-less PERSPECTIVE — FIXED 10.76→43.81 dB (2026-07-26) f2c957e+a6b62fa
Resolved the "camera-less orthographic vs perspective" conflict by DECODING (not deferring — there is
no defer). Controlled headless-FCP probes proved FCP applies PERSPECTIVE to camera-less scenes:
  • Position-Z plate shrinks: scale=d/(d−Z), 5 pts (Z-150→0.940 … -900→0.721) fit d=(W/2)/tan(22.5°),
    Motion default AOV=45°, =2317.6 for 1920w (scales with WIDTH: 1080w→~1304).
  • Rotation-X plate is a PERSPECTIVE TRAPEZOID (rotx60 top edge 1620 < bottom 1920) — NOT the flat
    rect orthographic would give. So the old evaluator distance=Infinity (ortho) for camera-less was
    WRONG vs headless; the "Fall prefers ortho" note measured wrong distances / a separate rotY-sign bug.
THREE decoded fixes (all probe-verified, RULE 2.0):
  (A) evaluator L460: negate posZ into m14 (Motion +Z = toward viewer; projectPoint uses cameraZ/(cameraZ+wz)).
  (B) perspective.ts: DEFAULT_CAMERA_Z + compositor cameraZ fallback → defaultCameraDistance(w)=(w/2)/tan(22.5°).
  (C) evaluator camera-less branch: return finite perspective distance (w/2)/tan(22.5°), NOT Infinity.
  (D) compositor: fill-conform A/B drop-zone plate to frame BEFORE projectQuad (FCP fills frame w/ media
      then 3D-transforms; engine was projecting native 1854 source → plate 1472 vs FCP 1526=1920×0.794).
RESULT: _t_3dr_v4 10.76 → 19.20 (A+B+C) → 43.81 dB (D); engine plate bbox x[197,1722] y[111,968] IDENTICAL
to FCP. Golden 2123/0, tsc clean. NOTE (RULE 2): may move Fall/Color_Planes/Reflection (were on the wrong
ortho default — their own separate bugs, not measured here per "don't chase regressions"). rotationY sign
is still latently inverted (not exercised by any passing slug; page-flip uses renderPageFlip). Probes /tmp/zprobe.

## Camera-less PERSPECTIVE — FIXED (2026-07-26) f2c957e + a6b62fa [_t_3dr_v4 10.76 → 43.81 dB]
RESOLVED the "camera-less orthographic vs perspective" conflict — FCP is PERSPECTIVE. Decoded via
controlled headless probes (5 Position-Z + rotX/rotY trapezoid), NOT deferred (RULE 1: finished the case).
  • FCP camera-less default = PERSPECTIVE at d = (frameWidth/2)/tan(AOV/2), Motion default AOV=45° → 2317.6
    for 1920w (5-pt fit 2324.8±7 ≡ 44.88°, scales with WIDTH). PROVEN perspective not ortho: rotX+60 plate
    is a foreshortened TRAPEZOID in FCP (top edge 1620 < bottom 1920) — ortho would be a flat 1920 rect.
    The old evaluator returned distance=Infinity (ortho) for camera-less → flat plate. The prior "Fall
    prefers orthographic" note measured wrong distances / a separate rotationY-sign residual; superseded.
  • Motion +Position-Z = TOWARD viewer; projectPoint uses cameraZ/(cameraZ+wz) (+wz=away), so posZ is
    NEGATED into m14 (evaluator L460). Reproduces all 5 probes <0.005 (Z-150→0.940 … -900→0.721). Rotation
    wz (R basis cols) unchanged — rotationX still matches via its existing -rotX negation.
  • FCP fill-conforms the A/B drop-zone plate to the FRAME (1920) BEFORE the 3D transform; the perspective
    branch was projecting the native 1854 source (plate 1472 vs FCP 1526). FIX: conformDropZoneSource to
    output before projectQuad, keyed on source.type transitionA/B (dropZone box dims may be stripped).
RESULT: _t_3dr_v4 plate bbox now IDENTICAL to FCP (x[197,1722] y[111,968]); 10.76→19.20→43.81 dB. Golden
2123/0, tsc clean. Files: evaluator/index.ts (posZ sign + camera-less finite d), perspective.ts
(defaultCameraDistance width-based), compositor/index.ts (cameraZ default + perspective conform).
NOTE: this likely moves Fall/Color_Planes/Reflection (were relying on the wrong ortho default) — per RULE 2
those are their own separate bugs, not measured here. PERSPECTIVE_Z_DECODE.md has the full probe tables.

## Switch/3D_Rect disabled-node semantics + parser robustness (2026-07-26) ab2c1e5,775317d
Re-minimized Movements/Switch (_t_switch_v2/v3) and re-scored 3D_Rect _t_3dr_v7. Decoded the
DISABLED-NODE model + three parser-robustness gaps (all exposed by minimizer name/ref stripping).

DISABLED-NODE MODEL (corrected):
 - A disabled node (<enabled>0</enabled>) contributes NO PIXELS, but its PARAMETER VALUES are still
   read as a Link/rig driver. i.e. enabled gates pixel OUTPUT, not the node's data as a controller.
 - ab2c1e5 (KEEP): a clone whose resolved SOURCE leaf is disabled renders nothing (no pixels to
   copy). _t_3dr_v7: clone chain 21867->14023->10009(disabled A) painted A full-frame, occluding the
   live receded-B clone; returning null when src.enabled===false → 8.89→43.81 dB.
 - 51a5c86 (REVERTED in 775317d): "a Link driven by a disabled source is inert" was WRONG. _t_switch_v3
   proves FCP READS the disabled Generator's Position X + Anchor X to drive Transition B's Links.

PARSER ROBUSTNESS (775317d) — three gaps that only bit after minimizer stripped names/refs:
 1. Link Source Object resolves by parameter id=201 (not name="Source Object"). v3's id=201 had no
    name → sourceObjectId=0 → both Links dropped → B rendered full-frame centred.
 2. Anchor Point container is id=107 NOT 106 (VERIFIED 19× name="Anchor Point" id="107", 0× at 106;
    106=3:2 Pulldown/Channel ID). Name lookup masked the wrong 106 fallback; stripped name →
    driver.transform.anchorX undefined → only Position link applied → B off-canvas at 2388 vs 1651.
 3. A transform Link expressionChannel with NEITHER <sourceChannelRef> NOR <channelBehavior
    affectingChannel> is inert (no source to copy). v2's stripped Link kept only <targetChannelID>1
    → old code fabricated position.X←position.X, read disabled driver posX=2388, pushed A off-canvas
    (BLACK). FCP renders A full-frame centred.

RESULT: _t_switch_v2 0.7→43.79 dB; _t_switch_v3 6.31→18.30 worst / 55.29 mean (residual = receded-tile
geometry, separate); _t_3dr_v7 8.89→43.81; golden 2123/0. NEXT: re-minimize original Movements/Switch
(0 disabled nodes) to isolate its own f23 A-vs-B tail-swap, a DIFFERENT cause from these repros.

## Switch disabled-node model + 3 parser-robustness fixes (2026-07-26) 775317d (reverts 51a5c86)
Re-minimized Movements/Switch → _t_switch_v2 (struct 5) and _t_switch_v3 (struct 6). v3 DISPROVED the
disabled-driver-link rule I'd shipped in 51a5c86, and exposed 3 parser gaps (all name/ref-stripping):

MODEL CORRECTION: a DISABLED node's PARAMETER VALUES are still read as a Link driver; only its PIXELS
are suppressed. v3 carries two COMPLETE Links (LinkPos.X ./1/100/101/1 + LinkAnchor.X ./1/100/107/1)
off the SAME disabled Generator and FCP APPLIES them (B → worldX = 2388−737 = 1651 = FCP content
left-edge x=1650). So 51a5c86 ("Link off a disabled source is inert") was WRONG → reverted. The
pixel-suppression rule ab2c1e5 (clone of a disabled source renders nothing) is CONSISTENT and stays
(_t_3dr_v7 holds 43.81 dB): enabled gates pixel OUTPUT, not parameter values.

PARSER FIXES (775317d):
 1. Link Source Object resolves by parameter id=201 (not name="Source Object"). v3's id=201 lost its
    name → sourceObjectId=0 → both Links DROPPED → B rendered full-frame centred.
 2. Anchor Point CONTAINER is id=107, NOT 106 (VERIFIED 19× name="Anchor Point" id="107" in real
    .motr, 0× at 106; 106=3:2 Pulldown/Channel ID). Name lookup masked the wrong 106 fallback; once
    the name was stripped, driver.transform.anchorX was undefined → only Position link applied → B
    off-canvas at 2388 vs 1651. NOTE: behaviors.ts already mapped path-107→'anchor'; only the
    transform CONTAINER extractor used the wrong 106.
 3. A transform Link expressionChannel with NEITHER <sourceChannelRef> NOR <channelBehavior
    affectingChannel> is inert. v2's stripped Link kept only <targetChannelID>1 → old code fabricated
    position.X←position.X, read the disabled driver's posX=2388, pushed Transition A off-canvas
    (BLACK). FCP renders A full-frame centred.

## Switch v4/v5 + minimizer Rig-Behavior protection (2026-07-26) 50018ab, 7c42e24
Continued the Switch minimize loop past v2/v3. Two more findings:

50018ab — transform Link requires a channelBehavior (target-property path). _t_switch_v4's minimized
Link kept <sourceChannelRef>./1/100/101/1 but LOST its <channelBehavior> → old code defaulted targetProp
to 'position' → copied the disabled driver's Position X=2388 onto Transition A's POSITION → A off-canvas
BLACK. FCP renders A full-frame centred. VERIFIED every genuine transform Link in all 65 FCP .motr pairs
a channelBehavior with sourceChannelRef; a Link missing it is a degenerate fragment → inert. Symmetric
with the no-source rule (775317d). _t_switch_v4 7.10→43.79 dB; golden 2123/0.

7c42e24 — minimizer protects Rig Behavior (factoryID 11). _t_switch_v5 exposed that stripping the rig
produces UNINTERPRETABLE fragments: a Rig Behavior sets the disabled driver's Position from a
per-direction Snapshots array (id=202, [2064/2363/2512]) via a Widget (id=200), and the Links read that
rig-driven channel. With the rig stripped, the ungated Links mis-fire (v5: engine ramps A off-frame /
darkens progressively while FCP holds A full-frame static). The Switch fragments v2..v5 each surfaced a
REAL parser bug (all now fixed), but further rig-stripping only yields more artifacts. Protecting Rig
Behaviors (always-on, same hazard class as referenced-node protection) keeps the rig intact so a
re-minimize isolates the GENUINE divergence.

FULL Switch real bug (unchanged by all the above; 0 disabled nodes, driven by Clone B + timing, NOT
Links): at f23 (t=1.6931s, last frame) the engine renders full-frame A [140.5,91,60] while FCP renders
full-frame B [100.6,115.8,147.7] (|D|=67.9). f0-f22 all match (A→B swap correct); only the final frame
flips back to A. Both Transition A (out=1.7017s) and Clone B (in=0.934 out=1.735s, clones Transition B)
are visible at f23; FCP shows Clone B on top, engine shows A. Evaluator state is IDENTICAL at f19/f22/f23
(both vis=1 op=1 tx=0) yet f19-f22 render B and f23 renders A → the flip is in the min-gen render path
(real media resolver), NOT the evaluator. NEXT: isolate via rig-protected re-minimize (_t_switch_v6).

## Movements/Switch SOLVED — clone-continuation wrap-cancel (2026-07-26) 6930343
After the v2-v5 parser fixes + Rig-Behavior minimizer protection, isolated the FULL Switch's real bug
(0 disabled nodes, driven by Clone B + timing, NOT Links): f0-f22 matched FCP but f23 flipped to
full-frame A [140,91,60] while FCP holds B [100,116,147] (|D|=67.9, worst 10.01 dB).

ROOT CAUSE (timemap.ts retime-wrap): wrapSec = Transition A's out (1.702s, retimeExtrap=1). The wrap
freezes the scene to frame 0 (re-show source A) once A times out. But Switch's "Clone B" clones
Transition B and lives to 1.735s ≈ endSec, so INCOMING B persists to the end — the transition settles
on B, NOT a loop to A. The half-frame wrap tolerance tripped the wrap at f23 (t=1.693 < A.out) →
frozen frame-0 A. The evaluator state at f19/f22/f23 is IDENTICAL (both A + Clone B visible op=1); the
flip was purely the timeMap.remap wrapping f23's time to 0.

FIX: track cloneContinuationOutSec (latest Clone Layer out) and cancel the wrap when a Clone continues
content to ≈endSec (>= endSec - 1 frame) — joins the pureCrossfadeSettleB / framing-camera / overlay
wrap-cancel family. Structural (clone lifetime vs endSec), no slug names.

RESULT: Movements__Switch 10.01 → 44.78 dB mean / 43.79 worst — ALL 24 frames match FCP (|D|~1.5).
Golden 2123/0; tsc clean. This closes the Switch investigation that spanned v2-v6 (5 shipped fixes:
clone-of-disabled, Source Object id=201, Anchor id=107, no-source Link inert, no-channelBehavior Link
inert, Rig-Behavior minimizer protection, and this clone-continuation wrap-cancel).

## Movements/Switch SOLVED — clone-continuation wrap-cancel (2026-07-26) 6930343
The full Switch f23 A-vs-B tail-swap ROOT-CAUSED and FIXED (10.01 → 44.78 dB, ALL frames match FCP).

DECODE (timemap.ts retime-wrap): Switch's OUTGOING Transition A carries retimingExtrapolation=1 with
out=1.702s, which set wrapSec=1.702. The remap wraps any frame at t ≥ wrapSec − half-frame (1.685s)
back to time 0 (re-showing source A). f23 sits at t=1.693s (progress 23/24 × endSec 1.767), so it
tripped the wrap → engine rendered full-frame A [140,91,60], while FCP holds full-frame B
[100,116,147] (|D|=67.9). f0-f22 already matched (A early; B mid via Clone B). The evaluator state at
f23 is IDENTICAL to f19/f22 (Clone B + Transition A both vis=1 op=1) — the divergence was purely the
time-map wrap, not z-order or visibility.

WHY the wrap is wrong for Switch: 'Clone B' clones Transition B and stays alive to out=1.735s ≈ endSec
(1.767 − 1 frame). The INCOMING B content therefore persists through and past A's timeout — the
transition SETTLES on B, it does NOT loop back to source A. The existing clonedContinuationSourceIds
logic already excluded Transition B from the wrap-MIN (so wrapSec came from A, not B), but nothing
CANCELLED the A-driven wrap.

FIX: track the latest Clone Layer `out` (cloneContinuationOutSec) and add cloneContinuesToEnd
(cloneContinuationOutSec ≥ endSec − frameSec) to the wrap-cancel OR-gate (alongside pureCrossfadeSettleB
/ framing-camera / overlay cancels). When a Clone keeps content alive to the animation end, the tail is
the cloned content, not a loop-to-A. Structural (clone lifetime vs endSec), no slug names.

RESULT: Movements__Switch 10.01 → 44.78 dB mean / 43.79 worst; every frame |D|~1.5. Golden 2123/0; tsc
clean. Switch is now essentially matched. Also landed this stretch: minimizer Rig-Behavior protection
(7c42e24) so future rig re-minimizes stay faithful.

## Minimizer protections REMOVED + FCP 720x486 default-format decoded (2026-07-26) b00b138, 61d996d
POLICY CHANGE (vjeux): the minimizer must strip EVERYTHING; match FCP on every input incl. degenerate
cases. Removed ALL always-on minimizer protections (referenced Source Media/Mask Source/Clone Target,
Rig Behavior, drop-zone A/B Type/Drop-Zone binding, <enabled>0>, scene-geometry, <factory> rows, clip
media refs, <vertex index=>). When a strip makes the engine diverge from FCP-headless, that IS the next
bug: RE what FCP renders for the stripped input and match it. Kept only: explicit --protect opt-in, the
ddmin render gate (unparseable reverts), and _KEEP_ATTRS parse-identity. Updated LOOP.md + minimize.py
docstring. (b00b138)

FIRST BUG the protection-free minimizer surfaced — FCP DEFAULT PROJECT FORMAT (61d996d):
Re-minimized Wipes/Diagonal with no protections → _t_diag_noprotect (174->3 struct, 28 lines,
sceneSettings fully STRIPPED). DECODED via FCP-headless probes: FCP honors authored <width>x<height>
ONLY when sceneSettings has ALL of {width,height,pixelAspectRatio,frameRate}; else it renders on the
720x486 (NTSC-DV) default canvas (duration NOT required). Probe table in the commit. Engine used to
default missing dims to 1920x1080 → dim mismatch vs FCP. Parser now resolves 720x486 for incomplete
formats. _t_diag_noprotect dim-mismatch fixed, 19.28 dB. Golden 2123/0.

RESIDUAL (open, RULE 1): _t_diag_noprotect still diverges on drop-zone CONFORM into the 720x486 4:3
canvas — FCP places the (empty-clip) drop-zone as a small centered ~284x257 region (20.8% coverage,
color [178,149,158]) while the engine conforms it larger; FCP also blacks out by f18 vs engine f23
(tail timing). Next: decode FCP's drop-zone placement/size for an empty clip in a DV-default scene.

  required. Engine defaulted missing dims to 1920x1080 -> divergence. Parser now resolves 720x486 for
  incomplete formats (61d996d). Golden 2123/0.
